To create an extension for Visual Studio Code that includes high contrast themes and outputs the `package.json` and `code` files, follow these steps:

### Step 1: Create a New VS Code Extension

First, you need to set up a new VS Code extension. You can do this by running the following command in your terminal:

```sh
vsce create my-high-contrast-theme
```

This will create a new directory with the structure of an extension project.

### Step 2: Add High Contrast Themes

In the `src` directory, you'll find several files and directories. You need to modify these files to include high contrast themes.

#### `theme.json`

Update the `package.json` file to specify the theme details:

```json
{
    "name": "high-contrast-theme",
    "version": "1.0.0",
    "description": "High contrast theme for Visual Studio Code",
    "author": {
        "name": "Your Name",
        "url": "https://yourwebsite.com"
    },
    "engines": {
        "vscode": "^1.65.0"
    },
    "categories": ["Themes"],
    "main": "index.js",
    "contributes": {
        "themes": [
            {
                "id": "high-contrast-theme",
                "label": "High Contrast Theme",
                "description": "A high contrast theme for Visual Studio Code.",
                "icon": {
                    "light": "images/high-contrast-icon-light.png",
                    "dark": "images/high-contrast-icon-dark.png"
                },
                "colorThemes": [
                    {
                        "scheme": "highContrast",
                        "colors": {
                            "foreground": "#FFFFFF",
                            "background": "#000000",
                            // Add your high contrast colors here
                        }
                    }
                ]
            }
        ]
    }
}
```

#### `src/index.js`

Create a new file called `index.js` in the root directory of the extension and add the following code to activate the theme:

```javascript
const vscode = require('vscode');

function activate(context) {
    // Add any initialization code here
}

exports.activate = activate;
```

#### `src/icons/high-contrast-icon-light.png` and `src/icons/high-contrast-icon-dark.png`

Create two images named `high-contrast-icon-light.png` and `high-contrast-icon-dark.png` in the `images` directory to represent the light and dark versions of your high contrast theme icon.

### Step 3: Package the Extension

To package the extension, run the following command:

```sh
vsce package
```

This will create a ZIP file named `my-high-contrast-theme.vsix` that contains all the necessary files for the extension.

### Step 4: Test the Extension

To test the extension, follow these steps:

1. Unzip the `my-high-contrast-theme.vsix` file.
2. Open Visual Studio Code and go to the Extensions view (`Ctrl+Shift+X`).
3. Click on the "Load from disk" button in the sidebar.
4. Navigate to the unzipped folder and select the extension.

Now you should have the high contrast theme installed and ready to use in Visual Studio Code.

### Step 5: Output `package.json` and `code`

You can output the `package.json` and `code` files directly from your VS Code environment using the following commands:

```sh
# Output package.json
npm list --depth=0 > package.json

# Output code
code --list-extensions | findstr high-contrast-theme
```

These commands will display the installed extensions and their versions, which you can use to verify that the extension is correctly installed.
